Abstract\n\t\t\t\t\t85 container\n\t\t\t\t\t\t86 text This paper presents a field-oriented methodology for modeling and controlling pressure regulation, demonstrated on a real water distribution network (WDN) sector. The aim is to provide a practical and reproducible pathway from field measurements to deployable real-time pressure control, addressing the gap between simulator-based numerical studies and full-scale field implementations. The study covers data-driven dynamic modeling, controller design, and pre-deployment performance assessment using real operational data. A low-order linear dynamic model with transport delay is identified directly from field measurements, combining 20 days of pressure and inflow data with step-response tests performed on an existing pressure-reducing valve (PRV). The model is validated against an independent 24-h dataset of real consumption, achieving a maximum deviation of approximately \n\t\t\t\t\t\t87 container\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t88 text ± 1 m\n\t\t\t\t\t\t89 container\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t90 text H\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t91 text 2\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t92 text O\n\t\t\t\t\t\t93 text  at the remote critical node, which is adequate for control-oriented modeling and controller synthesis. Based on the identified model, a digital reference signal tracking (RST) controller with a Smith predictor is designed using explicit performance specifications for settling time, overshoot, and bandwidth. The controller is evaluated in silico using real consumption data not used during identification, providing an unbiased assessment of closed-loop performance under representative demand variability. Results show that the proposed strategy maintains pressure at the remote critical node within acceptable bounds while effectively rejecting demand-induced disturbances. Although closed-loop field implementation is beyond the scope of this study, the framework is explicitly conceived as a preparatory step for real-time deployment. The main conclusion is that a transparent, data-driven methodology grounded in field measurements can reduce modeling and tuning effort while yielding controller designs compatible with standard programmable logic controller (PLC) and supervisory control and data acquisition (SCADA) platforms, enabling utilities to progress systematically toward practical real-time pressure control.\n\t\t\t\t94 container executive-summary\n\t\t\t\t\t95 heading Practical Applications, Value: 2\n\t\t\t\t\t\t96 text Practical Applications\n\t\t\t\t\t97 text This study offers practical guidance for water utilities seeking to improve pressure management while reducing water losses and infrastructure wear. Using real operational data, the proposed approach shows how effective pressure regulation strategies can be derived directly from field measurements and implemented with standard PLC and SCADA systems already used by most utilities. As a result, the methodology is accessible without requiring advanced hydraulic-modeling expertise or specialized simulation tools. Pressure management is particularly important because daily demand variations often produce excessive pressures during low consumption periods (e.g., nighttime). These pressure surges increase the likelihood of pipe bursts, background leakage, and premature deterioration of network assets. The proposed control strategy addresses this issue by stabilizing pressure at a critical point in the network, allowing utilities to maintain pressures within acceptable and regulatory limits despite changing demand. While the controller has not yet been deployed in closed-loop field operation, the methodology is designed as a practical step toward real-time implementation. By reducing modeling effort and simplifying controller tuning, the approach lowers technical barriers for utilities interested in advanced pressure control and provides a realistic foundation for improving network reliability and reducing operational costs.\n\t\t\t\t98 container Get full access to this article\n\t\t\t\t\t99 heading Get full access to this article, Value: 2, ID: denial-block-title\n\t\t\t\t\t\t100 text Get full access to this article\n\t\t\t\t\t101 text View all available purchase options and get full access to this article.\n\t\t\t\t\t102 link Description: GET ACCESS, Value: ascelibrary.org/doi…\n\t\t\t\t\t103 container\n\t\t\t\t\t\t104 text ALREADY A SUBSCRIBER? The proposed control strategy addresses this issue by stabilizing pressure at a critical point in the network, allowing utilities to maintain pressures within acceptable and regulatory limits despite changing demand. While the controller has not yet been deployed in closed-loop field operation, the methodology is designed as a practical step toward real-time implementation. By reducing modeling effort and simplifying controller tuning, the approach lowers technical barriers for utilities interested in advanced pressure control and provides a realistic foundation for improving network reliability and reducing operational costs.\n    - region \"Get full access to this article\":\n      - heading \"Get full access to this article\" [level=2]\n      - paragraph: View all available purchase options and get full access to this article.\n      - link \"Get Access\":\n        - /url: \"#core-collateral-purchase-access\"\n        - generic: Get Access\n      - link \"institution\":\n    - text: . ascelibrary.org/doi…, Secondary Actions: Expand\n\t\t\t\t88 container abstract\n\t\t\t\t\t89 heading Abstract, Value: 2\n\t\t\t\t\t\t90 text Abstract\n\t\t\t\t\t91 container\n\t\t\t\t\t\t92 text This paper presents a field-oriented methodology for modeling and controlling pressure regulation, demonstrated on a real water distribution network (WDN) sector. The aim is to provide a practical and reproducible pathway from field measurements to deployable real-time pressure control, addressing the gap between simulator-based numerical studies and full-scale field implementations. The study covers data-driven dynamic modeling, controller design, and pre-deployment performance assessment using real operational data. A low-order linear dynamic model with transport delay is identified directly from field measurements, combining 20 days of pressure and inflow data with step-response tests performed on an existing pressure-reducing valve (PRV). The model is validated against an independent 24-h dataset of real consumption, achieving a maximum deviation of approximately \n\t\t\t\t\t\t93 container plus or minus 1 normal m, ID: i001\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t94 container plus or minus 1 normal m\n\t\t\t\t\t\t95 container normal upper H 2 normal upper O, ID: i002\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t96 container normal upper H 2 normal upper O\n\t\t\t\t\t\t97 text  at the remote critical node, which is adequate for control-oriented modeling and controller synthesis. Based on the identified model, a digital reference signal tracking (RST) controller with a Smith predictor is designed using explicit performance specifications for settling time, overshoot, and bandwidth. The controller is evaluated in silico using real consumption data not used during identification, providing an unbiased assessment of closed-loop performance under representative demand variability. Results show that the proposed strategy maintains pressure at the remote critical node within acceptable bounds while effectively rejecting demand-induced disturbances. Although closed-loop field implementation is beyond the scope of this study, the framework is explicitly conceived as a preparatory step for real-time deployment. The main conclusion is that a transparent, data-driven methodology grounded in field measurements can reduce modeling and tuning effort while yielding controller designs compatible with standard programmable logic controller (PLC) and supervisory control and data acquisition (SCADA) platforms, enabling utilities to progress systematically toward practical real-time pressure control.\n\t\t\t\t98 container executive-summary\n\t\t\t\t\t99 heading Practical Applications, Value: 2\n\t\t\t\t\t\t100 text Practical Applications\n\t\t\t\t\t101 text This study offers practical guidance for water utilities seeking to improve pressure management while reducing water losses and infrastructure wear. Using real operational data, the proposed approach shows how effective pressure regulation strategies can be derived directly from field measurements and implemented with standard PLC and SCADA systems already used by most utilities. As a result, the methodology is accessible without requiring advanced hydraulic-modeling expertise or specialized simulation tools. Pressure management is particularly important because daily demand variations often produce excessive pressures during low consumption periods (e.g., nighttime). These pressure surges increase the likelihood of pipe bursts, background leakage, and premature deterioration of network assets. The proposed control strategy addresses this issue by stabilizing pressure at a critical point in the network, allowing utilities to maintain pressures within acceptable and regulatory limits despite changing demand. While the controller has not yet been deployed in closed-loop field operation, the methodology is designed as a practical step toward real-time implementation. By reducing modeling effort and simplifying controller tuning, the approach lowers technical barriers for utilities interested in advanced pressure control and provides a realistic foundation for improving network reliability and reducing operational costs.\n\t\t\t\t102 container Get full access to this article\n\t\t\t\t\t103 heading Get full access to this article, Value: 2, ID: denial-block-title\n\t\t\t\t\t\t104 text Get full access to this article\n\t\t\t\t\t105 text View all available purchase options and get full access to this article.\n\t\t\t\t\t106 link Description: GET ACCESS, Value: ascelibrary.org/doi…\n\t\t\t\t\t107 container\n\t\t\t\t\t\t108 text ALREADY A SUBSCRIBER? ascelibrary.org/doi…, Secondary Actions: Expand\n\t\t\t\t88 container abstract\n\t\t\t\t\t89 heading Abstract, Value: 2\n\t\t\t\t\t\t90 text Abstract\n\t\t\t\t\t91 container\n\t\t\t\t\t\t92 text This paper presents a field-oriented methodology for modeling and controlling pressure regulation, demonstrated on a real water distribution network (WDN) sector. The aim is to provide a practical and reproducible pathway from field measurements to deployable real-time pressure control, addressing the gap between simulator-based numerical studies and full-scale field implementations. The study covers data-driven dynamic modeling, controller design, and pre-deployment performance assessment using real operational data. A low-order linear dynamic model with transport delay is identified directly from field measurements, combining 20 days of pressure and inflow data with step-response tests performed on an existing pressure-reducing valve (PRV). The model is validated against an independent 24-h dataset of real consumption, achieving a maximum deviation of approximately \n\t\t\t\t\t\t93 container plus or minus 1 normal m, ID: i001\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t94 container plus or minus 1 normal m\n\t\t\t\t\t\t95 container normal upper H 2 normal upper O, ID: i002\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t96 container normal upper H 2 normal upper O\n\t\t\t\t\t\t97 text  at the remote critical node, which is adequate for control-oriented modeling and controller synthesis. Based on the identified model, a digital reference signal tracking (RST) controller with a Smith predictor is designed using explicit performance specifications for settling time, overshoot, and bandwidth. The controller is evaluated in silico using real consumption data not used during identification, providing an unbiased assessment of closed-loop performance under representative demand variability. Results show that the proposed strategy maintains pressure at the remote critical node within acceptable bounds while effectively rejecting demand-induced disturbances. Although closed-loop field implementation is beyond the scope of this study, the framework is explicitly conceived as a preparatory step for real-time deployment. The main conclusion is that a transparent, data-driven methodology grounded in field measurements can reduce modeling and tuning effort while yielding controller designs compatible with standard programmable logic controller (PLC) and supervisory control and data acquisition (SCADA) platforms, enabling utilities to progress systematically toward practical real-time pressure control.\n\t\t\t\t98 container executive-summary\n\t\t\t\t\t99 heading Practical Applications, Value: 2\n\t\t\t\t\t\t100 text Practical Applications\n\t\t\t\t\t101 text This study offers practical guidance for water utilities seeking to improve pressure management while reducing water losses and infrastructure wear. Using real operational data, the proposed approach shows how effective pressure regulation strategies can be derived directly from field measurements and implemented with standard PLC and SCADA systems already used by most utilities. As a result, the methodology is accessible without requiring advanced hydraulic-modeling expertise or specialized simulation tools. Pressure management is particularly important because daily demand variations often produce excessive pressures during low consumption periods (e.g., nighttime). These pressure surges increase the likelihood of pipe bursts, background leakage, and premature deterioration of network assets. The proposed control strategy addresses this issue by stabilizing pressure at a critical point in the network, allowing utilities to maintain pressures within acceptable and regulatory limits despite changing demand. While the controller has not yet been deployed in closed-loop field operation, the methodology is designed as a practical step toward real-time implementation. By reducing modeling effort and simplifying controller tuning, the approach lowers technical barriers for utilities interested in advanced pressure control and provides a realistic foundation for improving network reliability and reducing operational costs.\n\t\t\t\t102 container Get full access to this article\n\t\t\t\t\t103 heading Get full access to this article, Value: 2, ID: denial-block-title\n\t\t\t\t\t\t104 text Get full access to this article\n\t\t\t\t\t105 text View all available purchase options and get full access to this article.\n\t\t\t\t\t106 link Description: GET ACCESS, Value: ascelibrary.org/doi…\n\t\t\t\t\t107 container\n\t\t\t\t\t\t108 text ALREADY A SUBSCRIBER? ASCE will always consult with authors prior to retracting a paper, but in cases where wrongdoing has been confirmed or ASCE faces potential legal repercussions, a retraction may be published without the author(s)' consent.\n\nAll corrections published are included in feeds to all abstracting and indexing services, allowing for quick updates. All corrections are published online and in print and are available for readers for free (no subscription or registration required).\n\n### Crossmark\n\nAs a member of Crossref, ASCE participates in Crossmark, a multipublisher initiative to provide a standard way for readers to locate the authoritative version of a document. The appearance of the Crossmark logo on a document indicates that ASCE is committed to maintaining the content it publishes and to alerting readers to changes if and when they occur.\n\nClicking the Crossmark logo on a document will tell the author its current status and may provide additional publication-record information about the document.
